           │ Write Python code that extends the `Module` class below to complete the following task.                                                                            │

           │ > For a railroad crossing a control system needs to be developed that on receipt of a signal indicating that a train is approaching closes the gates, and only     │
           │ opens these gates after the train has sent a signal indicating that it crossed the road. The requirement that should be met by the control system is that the      │
           │ gates are always closed when the train is crossing the road. The complete system consists of the three components Train, Gate, and Controller: Train || Gate ||    │
           │ Controller.  Model this system. You can assume that all trains pass the relevant track section in the same direction.  Detail: The states of the transition system │
           │ for the Train have the following intuitive meaning: in state "far" the train is not close to the crossing, in state "near" it is approaching the crossing and has  │
           │ just sent a signal to notify this, and in state "in" it is at the crossing. The states "up" and "down" of the Gate have the obvious interpretation. The controller │
           │ has 4 states 0, 1, 2 and 3. It changes from state 0 to 1 when the train is approaching, from state 1 to 2 when the gate is lowering, from state 2 to 3 when the    │
           │ train is exiting the crossing, and from state 3 to 0 when the gate is raising. The state changes of the Controller stand for handshaking with the trains (via the  │
           │ actions approach and exit) and the Gate (via the actions lower and raise via which the Controller causes the gate to close or to open, respectively). Use the      │
           │ variable names train_state, controller_state and gate_state.  Make sure that you satisfy the following specifications: Based on the natural language description   │
           │ provided, we can generate the following invariants for the system: 1. If the train is in state "in," then the gate must be in state "down": [ train\_state = "in"  │
           │ \Rightarrow gate\_state = "down" \] 2. When the train is approaching (train is in state "near"), the controller should transition from state 0 to state 1: [       │
           │ train\_state = "near" \Rightarrow controller\_state = 1 \] 3. When the gate is lowering (gate is in state "down"), the controller should transition from state 1   │
           │ to state 2: [ gate\_state = "down" \Rightarrow controller\_state = 2 \] 4. When the train is exiting the crossing (train is in state "far"), the controller should │
           │ transition from state 2 to state 3: [ train\_state = "far" \Rightarrow controller\_state = 3 \] 5. When the gate is raising (gate is in state "up"), the           │
           │ controller should transition from state 3 to state 0: [ gate\_state = "up" \Rightarrow controller\_state = 0 \] These invariants ensure that the system behaves as │
           │ intended, with the gates always closed when the train is crossing the road.                                                                                        │
           │                                                                                                                                                                    │
           │ Reply with your Python code inside one unique code block.                                                                                                          │

           │             return False                                                        │

           │ Make sure that your code extends the `Module` class below and that it completes the following task.                                                                │
           │                                                                                                                                                                    │
           │ > For a railroad crossing a control system needs to be developed that on receipt of a signal indicating that a train is approaching closes the gates, and only     │
           │ opens these gates after the train has sent a signal indicating that it crossed the road. The requirement that should be met by the control system is that the      │
           │ gates are always closed when the train is crossing the road. The complete system consists of the three components Train, Gate, and Controller: Train || Gate ||    │
           │ Controller.  Model this system. You can assume that all trains pass the relevant track section in the same direction.  Detail: The states of the transition system │
           │ for the Train have the following intuitive meaning: in state "far" the train is not close to the crossing, in state "near" it is approaching the crossing and has  │
           │ just sent a signal to notify this, and in state "in" it is at the crossing. The states "up" and "down" of the Gate have the obvious interpretation. The controller │
           │ has 4 states 0, 1, 2 and 3. It changes from state 0 to 1 when the train is approaching, from state 1 to 2 when the gate is lowering, from state 2 to 3 when the    │
           │ train is exiting the crossing, and from state 3 to 0 when the gate is raising. The state changes of the Controller stand for handshaking with the trains (via the  │
           │ actions approach and exit) and the Gate (via the actions lower and raise via which the Controller causes the gate to close or to open, respectively). Use the      │
           │ variable names train_state, controller_state and gate_state.  Make sure that you satisfy the following specifications: Based on the natural language description   │
           │ provided, we can generate the following invariants for the system: 1. If the train is in state "in," then the gate must be in state "down": [ train\_state = "in"  │
           │ \Rightarrow gate\_state = "down" \] 2. When the train is approaching (train is in state "near"), the controller should transition from state 0 to state 1: [       │
           │ train\_state = "near" \Rightarrow controller\_state = 1 \] 3. When the gate is lowering (gate is in state "down"), the controller should transition from state 1   │
           │ to state 2: [ gate\_state = "down" \Rightarrow controller\_state = 2 \] 4. When the train is exiting the crossing (train is in state "far"), the controller should │
           │ transition from state 2 to state 3: [ train\_state = "far" \Rightarrow controller\_state = 3 \] 5. When the gate is raising (gate is in state "up"), the           │
           │ controller should transition from state 3 to state 0: [ gate\_state = "up" \Rightarrow controller\_state = 0 \] These invariants ensure that the system behaves as │
           │ intended, with the gates always closed when the train is crossing the road.                                                                                        │
           │                                                                                                                                                                    │
           │ Reply with your Python code inside one unique code block.                                                                                                          │
           │                                                                                                                                                                    │

           │ class RailroadCrossing(Module):                                                                                     │
           │     """A module to represent a railroad crossing system with Train, Gate, and Controller components."""             │
           │                                                                                                                     │
           │     def types(self):                                                                                                │
           │         """Defines the types of the variables."""                                                                   │
           │         self.train_state = Enum(sort={'far', 'near', 'in'})                                                         │
           │         self.gate_state = Enum(sort={'up', 'down'})                                                                 │
           │         self.controller_state = Range(4)                                                                            │
           │                                                                                                                     │
           │     def locals(self):                                                                                               │
           │         """Defines the local variables."""                                                                          │
           │         self.train_state = self.train_state()                                                                       │
           │         self.gate_state = self.gate_state()                                                                         │
           │         self.controller_state = self.controller_state()                                                             │
           │                                                                                                                     │
           │     def init(self):                                                                                                 │
           │         """Initializes the variables."""                                                                            │
           │         self.train_state = self.train_state.enum_const("far")                                                       │
           │         self.gate_state = self.gate_state.enum_const("up")                                                          │
           │         self.controller_state = self.controller_state.const(0)                                                      │
           │                                                                                                                     │
           │     def next(self):                                                                                                 │
           │         """Defines the transition relation."""                                                                      │
           │         approach_trigger = And(self.controller_state == 0, self.train_state == 'near')                              │
           │         exit_trigger = And(self.controller_state == 2, self.train_state == 'in')                                    │
           │         lower_trigger = And(self.controller_state == 1, self.gate_state == 'up')                                    │
           │         raise_trigger = And(self.controller_state == 3, self.train_state == 'far', self.gate_state == 'down')       │
           │                                                                                                                     │
           │         self.train_state.next = If(Or(lower_trigger, raise_trigger), self.train_state,                              │
           │                                    If(self.train_state == 'far', 'near', 'in'))                                     │
           │         self.gate_state.next = If(Or(lower_trigger, raise_trigger), self.gate_state,                                │
           │                                   If(self.gate_state == 'up', 'down', 'up'))                                        │
           │         self.controller_state.next = If(approach_trigger, 1,                                                        │
           │                                         If(self.controller_state == 1, 2,                                           │
           │                                            If(exit_trigger, 3,                                                      │
           │                                               If(self.controller_state == 3, 0, self.controller_state))))           │
           │                                                                                                                     │
           │     def specification(self):                                                                                        │
           │         """Defines the specification based on invariants."""                                                        │
           │         invariant1 = Implies(And(self.controller_state == 1, self.train_state == 'near'),                           │
           │                              self.gate_state == 'down')                                                             │
           │         invariant2 = Implies(And(self.controller_state == 3, self.train_state == 'far', self.gate_state == 'down'), │
           │                              self.gate_state == 'up')                                                               │
           │         invariant3 = Implies(self.controller_state == 0, self.train_state == 'near')                                │
           │         invariant4 = Implies(self.controller_state == 1, self.gate_state == 'down')                                 │
           │         invariant5 = Implies(self.controller_state == 2, self.train_state == 'in')                                  │
           │                                                                                                                     │
           │         return And(invariant1, invariant2, invariant3, invariant4, invariant5)                                      │

           │ > For a railroad crossing a control system needs to be developed that on receipt of a signal indicating that a train is approaching closes the gates, and only     │
           │ opens these gates after the train has sent a signal indicating that it crossed the road. The requirement that should be met by the control system is that the      │
           │ gates are always closed when the train is crossing the road. The complete system consists of the three components Train, Gate, and Controller: Train || Gate ||    │
           │ Controller.  Model this system. You can assume that all trains pass the relevant track section in the same direction.  Detail: The states of the transition system │
           │ for the Train have the following intuitive meaning: in state "far" the train is not close to the crossing, in state "near" it is approaching the crossing and has  │
           │ just sent a signal to notify this, and in state "in" it is at the crossing. The states "up" and "down" of the Gate have the obvious interpretation. The controller │
           │ has 4 states 0, 1, 2 and 3. It changes from state 0 to 1 when the train is approaching, from state 1 to 2 when the gate is lowering, from state 2 to 3 when the    │
           │ train is exiting the crossing, and from state 3 to 0 when the gate is raising. The state changes of the Controller stand for handshaking with the trains (via the  │
           │ actions approach and exit) and the Gate (via the actions lower and raise via which the Controller causes the gate to close or to open, respectively). Use the      │
           │ variable names train_state, controller_state and gate_state. [Hint 1] Clarify in the task description that each transition from one controller state to another    │
           │ should explicitly check both the train's state and gate’s state to ensure proper sequencing and synchronization of operations (e.g., the controller should only    │
           │ transition to a state that lowers the gates if the train is 'near' and the gate is 'up'). [Hint 2] Amend the description to explicitly list the events that        │
           │ trigger the state transitions within the controller for more procedural clarity. Specify that the controller should transition from state 1 to state 2 only if it  │
           │ receives confirmation that the gate has transitioned to 'down', reflective of the 'lowering the gate' action. [Hint 3] Include a directive that the state          │
           │ transition from train state 'in' to 'near' should not occur in the model. This would help in maintaining consistency as logically, a train state should transition │

           │ transitions between active train states ('near' or 'in') or between gate states without going through the controller. This ensures the controller acts as the      │
           │ central coordinating entity, managing state transitions based on system-wide statuses.  [Hint 6] Adjust the description to include details about how the system    │
           │ should behave if the train transitions back to the 'far' state after crossing. Clarify that Controller should enter an idle state (e.g., state 0) after it has     │
           │ managed the crossing operation and returns to an initial state awaiting the next train approach signal.  [Hint 7] Detail out the necessary sequential logic links  │

           │ > For a railroad crossing a control system needs to be developed that on receipt of a signal indicating that a train is approaching closes the gates, and only     │
           │ opens these gates after the train has sent a signal indicating that it crossed the road. The requirement that should be met by the control system is that the      │
           │ gates are always closed when the train is crossing the road. The complete system consists of the three components Train, Gate, and Controller: Train || Gate ||    │
           │ Controller.  Model this system. You can assume that all trains pass the relevant track section in the same direction.  Detail: The states of the transition system │
           │ for the Train have the following intuitive meaning: in state "far" the train is not close to the crossing, in state "near" it is approaching the crossing and has  │
           │ just sent a signal to notify this, and in state "in" it is at the crossing. The states "up" and "down" of the Gate have the obvious interpretation. The controller │
           │ has 4 states 0, 1, 2 and 3. It changes from state 0 to 1 when the train is approaching, from state 1 to 2 when the gate is lowering, from state 2 to 3 when the    │
           │ train is exiting the crossing, and from state 3 to 0 when the gate is raising. The state changes of the Controller stand for handshaking with the trains (via the  │
           │ actions approach and exit) and the Gate (via the actions lower and raise via which the Controller causes the gate to close or to open, respectively). Use the      │
           │ variable names train_state, controller_state and gate_state. [Hint 1] Clarify in the task description that each transition from one controller state to another    │
           │ should explicitly check both the train's state and gate’s state to ensure proper sequencing and synchronization of operations (e.g., the controller should only    │
           │ transition to a state that lowers the gates if the train is 'near' and the gate is 'up'). [Hint 2] Amend the description to explicitly list the events that        │
           │ trigger the state transitions within the controller for more procedural clarity. Specify that the controller should transition from state 1 to state 2 only if it  │
           │ receives confirmation that the gate has transitioned to 'down', reflective of the 'lowering the gate' action. [Hint 3] Include a directive that the state          │
           │ transition from train state 'in' to 'near' should not occur in the model. This would help in maintaining consistency as logically, a train state should transition │
           │ from 'near' to 'in' and then to 'far'.  [Hint 4] Request to revise the description so it ensures 'raise' action by the controller only takes place when the train  │
           │ is confirmed far and the gate is 'down'. This step is vital to minimize the risk of the gates opening prematurely. [Hint 5] Emphasize in the task to avoid direct  │
           │ transitions between active train states ('near' or 'in') or between gate states without going through the controller. This ensures the controller acts as the      │
           │ central coordinating entity, managing state transitions based on system-wide statuses.  [Hint 6] Adjust the description to include details about how the system    │
           │ should behave if the train transitions back to the 'far' state after crossing. Clarify that Controller should enter an idle state (e.g., state 0) after it has     │
           │ managed the crossing operation and returns to an initial state awaiting the next train approach signal.  [Hint 7] Detail out the necessary sequential logic links  │
           │ between the gate’s movement and the train's position concerning controller states. This would involve specifying that gate operations should never commence unless │

           │ reliability at the railroad crossing. Make sure that you satisfy the following specifications: Based on the natural language description of the system involving   │

           │ > For a railroad crossing a control system needs to be developed that on receipt of a signal indicating that a train is approaching closes the gates, and only     │
           │ opens these gates after the train has sent a signal indicating that it crossed the road. The requirement that should be met by the control system is that the      │
           │ gates are always closed when the train is crossing the road. The complete system consists of the three components Train, Gate, and Controller: Train || Gate ||    │
           │ Controller.  Model this system. You can assume that all trains pass the relevant track section in the same direction.  Detail: The states of the transition system │
           │ for the Train have the following intuitive meaning: in state "far" the train is not close to the crossing, in state "near" it is approaching the crossing and has  │
           │ just sent a signal to notify this, and in state "in" it is at the crossing. The states "up" and "down" of the Gate have the obvious interpretation. The controller │
           │ has 4 states 0, 1, 2 and 3. It changes from state 0 to 1 when the train is approaching, from state 1 to 2 when the gate is lowering, from state 2 to 3 when the    │
           │ train is exiting the crossing, and from state 3 to 0 when the gate is raising. The state changes of the Controller stand for handshaking with the trains (via the  │
           │ actions approach and exit) and the Gate (via the actions lower and raise via which the Controller causes the gate to close or to open, respectively). Use the      │
           │ variable names train_state, controller_state and gate_state. [Hint 1] Clarify in the task description that each transition from one controller state to another    │
           │ should explicitly check both the train's state and gate’s state to ensure proper sequencing and synchronization of operations (e.g., the controller should only    │
           │ transition to a state that lowers the gates if the train is 'near' and the gate is 'up'). [Hint 2] Amend the description to explicitly list the events that        │
           │ trigger the state transitions within the controller for more procedural clarity. Specify that the controller should transition from state 1 to state 2 only if it  │
           │ receives confirmation that the gate has transitioned to 'down', reflective of the 'lowering the gate' action. [Hint 3] Include a directive that the state          │
           │ transition from train state 'in' to 'near' should not occur in the model. This would help in maintaining consistency as logically, a train state should transition │
           │ from 'near' to 'in' and then to 'far'.  [Hint 4] Request to revise the description so it ensures 'raise' action by the controller only takes place when the train  │

           │ transitions between active train states ('near' or 'in') or between gate states without going through the controller. This ensures the controller acts as the      │
           │ central coordinating entity, managing state transitions based on system-wide statuses.  [Hint 6] Adjust the description to include details about how the system    │
           │ should behave if the train transitions back to the 'far' state after crossing. Clarify that Controller should enter an idle state (e.g., state 0) after it has     │
           │ managed the crossing operation and returns to an initial state awaiting the next train approach signal.  [Hint 7] Detail out the necessary sequential logic links  │
           │ between the gate’s movement and the train's position concerning controller states. This would involve specifying that gate operations should never commence unless │
           │ coordinated with the train's position signalized through the controller unit. [Hint 8] Propose an error mitigation strategy in face of faulty state information —  │

           │ reliability at the railroad crossing. Make sure that you satisfy the following specifications: Based on the natural language description of the system involving   │
           │ the Train, Gate, and Controller components, the following invariants can be generated: 1. Invariant 1: The gates should only be lowered when the controller is in  │
           │ state 1 and the train is in the 'near' state. This can be expressed as: [ \forall train\_state, gate\_state, controller\_state: ((controller\_state = 1) \land     │
           │ (train\_state = 'near')) \Rightarrow (gate\_state = 'down') \] 2. Invariant 2: The gates should only be raised when the controller is in state 3, the train is in  │
           │ the 'far' state, and the gate is in the 'down' state. This can be expressed as: [ \forall train\_state, gate\_state, controller\_state: ((controller\_state = 3)   │

           │ for the Train have the following intuitive meaning: in state "far" the train is not close to the crossing, in state "near" it is approaching the crossing and has  │
           │ just sent a signal to notify this, and in state "in" it is at the crossing. The states "up" and "down" of the Gate have the obvious interpretation. The controller │
           │ has 4 states 0, 1, 2 and 3. It changes from state 0 to 1 when the train is approaching, from state 1 to 2 when the gate is lowering, from state 2 to 3 when the    │
           │ train is exiting the crossing, and from state 3 to 0 when the gate is raising. The state changes of the Controller stand for handshaking with the trains (via the  │
           │ actions approach and exit) and the Gate (via the actions lower and raise via which the Controller causes the gate to close or to open, respectively). Use the      │
           │ variable names train_state, controller_state and gate_state. [Hint 1] Clarify in the task description that each transition from one controller state to another    │
           │ should explicitly check both the train's state and gate’s state to ensure proper sequencing and synchronization of operations (e.g., the controller should only    │
           │ transition to a state that lowers the gates if the train is 'near' and the gate is 'up'). [Hint 2] Amend the description to explicitly list the events that        │
           │ trigger the state transitions within the controller for more procedural clarity. Specify that the controller should transition from state 1 to state 2 only if it  │
           │ receives confirmation that the gate has transitioned to 'down', reflective of the 'lowering the gate' action. [Hint 3] Include a directive that the state          │
           │ transition from train state 'in' to 'near' should not occur in the model. This would help in maintaining consistency as logically, a train state should transition │
           │ from 'near' to 'in' and then to 'far'.  [Hint 4] Request to revise the description so it ensures 'raise' action by the controller only takes place when the train  │
           │ is confirmed far and the gate is 'down'. This step is vital to minimize the risk of the gates opening prematurely. [Hint 5] Emphasize in the task to avoid direct  │
           │ transitions between active train states ('near' or 'in') or between gate states without going through the controller. This ensures the controller acts as the      │
           │ central coordinating entity, managing state transitions based on system-wide statuses.  [Hint 6] Adjust the description to include details about how the system    │
           │ should behave if the train transitions back to the 'far' state after crossing. Clarify that Controller should enter an idle state (e.g., state 0) after it has     │
           │ managed the crossing operation and returns to an initial state awaiting the next train approach signal.  [Hint 7] Detail out the necessary sequential logic links  │
           │ between the gate’s movement and the train's position concerning controller states. This would involve specifying that gate operations should never commence unless │
           │ coordinated with the train's position signalized through the controller unit. [Hint 8] Propose an error mitigation strategy in face of faulty state information —  │

           │ [Hint 9] Introduce a reminder for periodic system checks and maintenance routines within the system usage instructions to prevent state mismanagement and ensure   │
           │ technical efficiencies are maintained. By expanding the task description with these suggestions, the model’s alignment with the intended operational logic of the  │
           │ Railroad Crossing System can significantly improve. This will help avoid ambiguities and ensure that all components interact correctly to maintain safety and      │
           │ reliability at the railroad crossing. Make sure that you satisfy the following specifications: Based on the natural language description of the system involving   │
           │ the Train, Gate, and Controller components, the following invariants can be generated: 1. Invariant 1: The gates should only be lowered when the controller is in  │
           │ state 1 and the train is in the 'near' state. This can be expressed as: [ \forall train\_state, gate\_state, controller\_state: ((controller\_state = 1) \land     │
           │ (train\_state = 'near')) \Rightarrow (gate\_state = 'down') \] 2. Invariant 2: The gates should only be raised when the controller is in state 3, the train is in  │
           │ the 'far' state, and the gate is in the 'down' state. This can be expressed as: [ \forall train\_state, gate\_state, controller\_state: ((controller\_state = 3)   │
           │ \land (train\_state = 'far') \land (gate\_state = 'down')) \Rightarrow (gate\_state = 'up') \] 3. Invariant 3: The controller should transition from state 0 to    │
           │ state 1 only when the train is in the 'near' state. This can be expressed as: [ \forall train\_state, controller\_state: (controller\_state = 0) \Rightarrow       │
           │ (train\_state = 'near') \] 4. Invariant 4: The controller should transition from state 1 to state 2 only when the gate is in the 'down' state. This can be         │
           │ expressed as: [ \forall gate\_state, controller\_state: (controller\_state = 1) \Rightarrow (gate\_state = 'down') \] 5. Invariant 5: The controller should        │
           │ transition from state 2 to state 3 only when the train is in the 'in' state. This can be expressed as: [ \forall train\_state, controller\_state:                  │
           │ (controller\_state = 2) \Rightarrow (train\_state = 'in') \] By incorporating these invariants into the formal model of the Railroad Crossing System, the behavior │

           │ - If (controller_state = 0) and (train_state = near) and (gate_state = up), then (next_state = 1).  - If (controller_state = 1) and (train_state = near) and       │
           │ (gate_state = down), then (next_state = 2).  - If (controller_state = 2) and (train_state = in), then (next_state = 3).  - If (controller_state = 3) and           │
           │ (gate_state = up), then (next_state = 0). 2. Invariant 2:  The gates can only be raised (gate_state = up) if the train has crossed the road (train_state = far)    │
           │ and the controller state is 0. 3. Invariant 3:  The gates can only be lowered (gate_state = down) if the train is approaching (train_state = near) and the         │
           │ controller state is 1. 4. Invariant 4:  The train can only be at the crossing (train_state = in) if the controller state is 2. 5. Invariant 5:  Once the train has │
           │ crossed the road and is at a considerable distance (train_state = far), the controller should return to an idle state (controller_state = 0) to await the next     │
           │ train approach signal. These invariants ensure proper sequencing, synchronization, and safety in the Railroad Crossing System, maintaining the desired behavior    │
           │ where the gates are always closed when the train is crossing the road.                                                                                             │
           │                                                                                                                                                                    │
           │ Reply with your Python code inside one unique code block.                                                                                                          │

           │ Make sure that your code extends the `Module` class below and that it completes the following task.                                                                │
           │                                                                                                                                                                    │
           │ > For a railroad crossing a control system needs to be developed that on receipt of a signal indicating that a train is approaching closes the gates, and only     │
           │ opens these gates after the train has sent a signal indicating that it crossed the road. The requirement that should be met by the control system is that the      │
           │ gates are always closed when the train is crossing the road. The complete system consists of the three components Train, Gate, and Controller: Train || Gate ||    │
           │ Controller.  Model this system. You can assume that all trains pass the relevant track section in the same direction.  Detail: The states of the transition system │
           │ for the Train have the following intuitive meaning: in state "far" the train is not close to the crossing, in state "near" it is approaching the crossing and has  │
           │ just sent a signal to notify this, and in state "in" it is at the crossing. The states "up" and "down" of the Gate have the obvious interpretation. The controller │
           │ has 4 states 0, 1, 2 and 3. It changes from state 0 to 1 when the train is approaching, from state 1 to 2 when the gate is lowering, from state 2 to 3 when the    │
           │ train is exiting the crossing, and from state 3 to 0 when the gate is raising. The state changes of the Controller stand for handshaking with the trains (via the  │
           │ actions approach and exit) and the Gate (via the actions lower and raise via which the Controller causes the gate to close or to open, respectively). Use the      │
           │ variable names train_state, controller_state and gate_state. [Hint 1] Clarify in the task description that each transition from one controller state to another    │
           │ should explicitly check both the train's state and gate’s state to ensure proper sequencing and synchronization of operations (e.g., the controller should only    │
           │ transition to a state that lowers the gates if the train is 'near' and the gate is 'up'). [Hint 2] Amend the description to explicitly list the events that        │
           │ trigger the state transitions within the controller for more procedural clarity. Specify that the controller should transition from state 1 to state 2 only if it  │
           │ receives confirmation that the gate has transitioned to 'down', reflective of the 'lowering the gate' action. [Hint 3] Include a directive that the state          │
           │ transition from train state 'in' to 'near' should not occur in the model. This would help in maintaining consistency as logically, a train state should transition │
           │ from 'near' to 'in' and then to 'far'.  [Hint 4] Request to revise the description so it ensures 'raise' action by the controller only takes place when the train  │
           │ is confirmed far and the gate is 'down'. This step is vital to minimize the risk of the gates opening prematurely. [Hint 5] Emphasize in the task to avoid direct  │
           │ transitions between active train states ('near' or 'in') or between gate states without going through the controller. This ensures the controller acts as the      │
           │ central coordinating entity, managing state transitions based on system-wide statuses.  [Hint 6] Adjust the description to include details about how the system    │
           │ should behave if the train transitions back to the 'far' state after crossing. Clarify that Controller should enter an idle state (e.g., state 0) after it has     │

           │ > For a railroad crossing a control system needs to be developed that on receipt of a signal indicating that a train is approaching closes the gates, and only     │
           │ opens these gates after the train has sent a signal indicating that it crossed the road. The requirement that should be met by the control system is that the      │
           │ gates are always closed when the train is crossing the road. The complete system consists of the three components Train, Gate, and Controller: Train || Gate ||    │
           │ Controller.  Model this system. You can assume that all trains pass the relevant track section in the same direction.  Detail: The states of the transition system │
           │ for the Train have the following intuitive meaning: in state "far" the train is not close to the crossing, in state "near" it is approaching the crossing and has  │
           │ just sent a signal to notify this, and in state "in" it is at the crossing. The states "up" and "down" of the Gate have the obvious interpretation. The controller │
           │ has 4 states 0, 1, 2 and 3. It changes from state 0 to 1 when the train is approaching, from state 1 to 2 when the gate is lowering, from state 2 to 3 when the    │
           │ train is exiting the crossing, and from state 3 to 0 when the gate is raising. The state changes of the Controller stand for handshaking with the trains (via the  │
           │ actions approach and exit) and the Gate (via the actions lower and raise via which the Controller causes the gate to close or to open, respectively). Use the      │
           │ variable names train_state, controller_state and gate_state. [Hint 1] Define clear variable state values for clarity and precision: The task description should    │
           │ specify what integer values the TrainState, GateState, and ControllerState variables correspond to. For example, TrainState: 0 for 'far', 1 for 'near', and 2 for  │
           │ 'in'; GateState: 0 for 'up' and 1 for 'down'; ControllerState: 0-3 representing each state transition sequence. [Hint 2] Detail the gate operations and            │
           │ controller's response: The task should explicitly mention that the gate must start closing (state transition from 'up' to 'down') when the controller state        │
           │ transitions from 0 to 1 upon train approaching. Additionally, the gate should only open ('down' to 'up') once the controller state goes from 3 back to 0 after the │
           │ train has exited the crossing. [Hint 3] Clarify the train signal handling: Include in the task that the controller should transition from its initial state (0) to │
           │ 1 when it receives an 'approaching' signal (train_state changes to 'near') and must not transition to state 2 until the gate is fully closed (gate_state is        │
           │ 'down'). [Hint 4] Ensure explicit error handling or state constraints: The task description should specify what happens if the train reaches the crossing          │
           │ (train_state = 'in') but the gate is not yet fully down (gate_state != 'down'), implying a fail-safe mechanism needs to be detailed. [Hint 5] Sequential           │
           │ correctness and state transitions: It should be clearly stated that the state transitions within the controller are strictly sequential following the order: 0 ->  │
           │ 1 -> 2 -> 3 -> 0, ensuring that each state is a prerequisite for the next and only transitions upon completion of the required conditions like gate movement or    │

           │ debugging and simulation process of the controller system. Make sure that you satisfy the following specifications: Invariants: 1. train_state ∈ {0, 1, 2} and     │

           │ Write Python code that extends the `Module` class below to complete the following task.                                                                            │
           │                                                                                                                                                                    │
           │ > For a railroad crossing a control system needs to be developed that on receipt of a signal indicating that a train is approaching closes the gates, and only     │
           │ opens these gates after the train has sent a signal indicating that it crossed the road. The requirement that should be met by the control system is that the      │
           │ gates are always closed when the train is crossing the road. The complete system consists of the three components Train, Gate, and Controller: Train || Gate ||    │
           │ Controller.  Model this system. You can assume that all trains pass the relevant track section in the same direction.  Detail: The states of the transition system │
           │ for the Train have the following intuitive meaning: in state "far" the train is not close to the crossing, in state "near" it is approaching the crossing and has  │
           │ just sent a signal to notify this, and in state "in" it is at the crossing. The states "up" and "down" of the Gate have the obvious interpretation. The controller │
           │ has 4 states 0, 1, 2 and 3. It changes from state 0 to 1 when the train is approaching, from state 1 to 2 when the gate is lowering, from state 2 to 3 when the    │
           │ train is exiting the crossing, and from state 3 to 0 when the gate is raising. The state changes of the Controller stand for handshaking with the trains (via the  │
           │ actions approach and exit) and the Gate (via the actions lower and raise via which the Controller causes the gate to close or to open, respectively). Use the      │
           │ variable names train_state, controller_state and gate_state. [Correction 1] Specify the required invariant conditions for the model clearly. Define that the       │
           │ primary invariant must check that the gates must be closed ("down" which is represented by `gate_state = 1`) whenever the train is in the crossing state ("in"     │
           │ which is represented by `train_state = BitVectorVal(2, 2)`). This will directly tie the required properties to their implementation in the model. [Correction 2]   │
           │ Expand the description of controller states to explicitly state their role in coordinating between the train and gate states. Specify that state 0 indicates       │
           │ waiting, state 1 indicates a train is approaching, state 2 indicates the train is at the crossing with gates closed, and state 3 indicates the train has passed    │
           │ and the gates can be opened. This granularity ensures model actions are triggered by clear, described conditions. [Correction 3] Describe the exact triggering     │
           │ conditions and allowable transitions between the states of the controller, train, and gate. For example, explain that the controller moves from state 0 to state 1 │
           │ when it receives a signal that the train is near (i.e., `train_state` changes to near). This prevents ambiguous transitions and maintains system consistency.      │
           │ [Correction 4] Include a requirement that all transitions must ensure gate safety, clearly instructing the implementation to verify these conditions rigorously in │
           │ automated tests. Expand upon the importance of gates being in the correct state corresponding to the train’s proximity and crossing status to ensure no safety     │
           │ protocols are violated. [Correction 5] Provide definitions for what each invariant (from `invariant1` to `invariant9`) is supposed to represent. This structure    │

           │ > For a railroad crossing a control system needs to be developed that on receipt of a signal indicating that a train is approaching closes the gates, and only     │
           │ opens these gates after the train has sent a signal indicating that it crossed the road. The requirement that should be met by the control system is that the      │
           │ gates are always closed when the train is crossing the road. The complete system consists of the three components Train, Gate, and Controller: Train || Gate ||    │
           │ Controller.  Model this system. You can assume that all trains pass the relevant track section in the same direction.  Detail: The states of the transition system │
           │ for the Train have the following intuitive meaning: in state "far" the train is not close to the crossing, in state "near" it is approaching the crossing and has  │
           │ just sent a signal to notify this, and in state "in" it is at the crossing. The states "up" and "down" of the Gate have the obvious interpretation. The controller │
           │ has 4 states 0, 1, 2 and 3. It changes from state 0 to 1 when the train is approaching, from state 1 to 2 when the gate is lowering, from state 2 to 3 when the    │
           │ train is exiting the crossing, and from state 3 to 0 when the gate is raising. The state changes of the Controller stand for handshaking with the trains (via the  │
           │ actions approach and exit) and the Gate (via the actions lower and raise via which the Controller causes the gate to close or to open, respectively). Use the      │
           │ variable names train_state, controller_state and gate_state. [Hint 1] Update the state descriptions for the Train. The descriptions for states such as 'far',      │
           │ 'near', and 'in' should include clear indication about the distances from the crossing or how the proximity is determined. These details are crucial for           │
           │ controlling the gate perfectly aligned with the train's position. [Hint 2] Define the states "up" and "down" for the Gate more explicitly by specifying the        │
           │ conditions under which these states are achieved. For example, detailing the level to which the gate is lowered or raised and including any intermediate states or │
           │ delays that might be inherent in the gate's operation. [Hint 3] Redefine state transitions in the Controller to include conditions for each transition from one    │
           │ state to another. This includes specifying exact conditions when the controller should move the gate to lowering or raising positions according to the signals     │
           │ received from the Train. [Hint 4] Add constraints to specify that when the train is in the "in" state, the gate must always be in the "down" state. This           │
           │ reinforces the safety requirement that gates are closed when the train is crossing the road. [Hint 5] Elaborate on the handshaking mechanism between the Train,    │
           │ Gate, and Controller. This should detail out the signal exchange process that marks the beginning and end of a train's crossing, ensuring each component's state   │
           │ aligns systematically with the other's. [Hint 6] Clarify what values the variables (train_state, controller_state and gate_state) hold for each condition or state │
           │ mentioned, ensuring that it's easy to map these variables to their descriptions throughout the system for better understanding and troubleshooting. [Hint 7]       │
           │ Integrate a fail-safe mechanism or exception handling description that details what happens in cases of signal failure or miscommunication among the Train, Gate,  │
           │ and Controller, ensuring safety isn't compromised. [Hint 8] Include timings or thresholds if relevant, such as the time taken for the gate to lower once the       │
           │ signal is received, or how long after a train has passed that the gates can be safely raised. These timings can be crucial for system stability and safety. [Hint  │
           │ 9] Define how the system should behave when the gate is in transition between states (moving from up to down and vice versa) and how these transitions are         │
           │ controlled by the Controller in response to the Train's states. Make sure that you satisfy the following specifications: Based on the provided natural language    │
           │ description for the railroad crossing control system, the following invariants can be generated for the system: 1. When the train is in the "in" state, the gate   │
           │ must always be in the "down" state: [ (train\_state = in) \Rightarrow (gate\_state = down) \] 2. The controller state changes from 0 to 1 when the train is        │
           │ approaching, from 1 to 2 when the gate is lowering, from 2 to 3 when the train is exiting the crossing, and from 3 to 0 when the gate is raising: [                │
           │ (controller\_state = 0) \Rightarrow (train\_state = near) \] [ (controller\_state = 1) \Rightarrow (gate\_state = down) \] [ (controller\_state = 2) \Rightarrow   │
           │ (train\_state = in) \] [ (controller\_state = 3) \Rightarrow (gate\_state = up) \] 3. Clear indication of the distances from the crossing or how proximity is      │
           │ determined for the train states "far", "near", and "in": [ (train\_state = far) \Rightarrow \text{(distance measurement)} \] [ (train\_state = near) \Rightarrow   │
           │ \text{(distance measurement)} \] [ (train\_state = in) \Rightarrow \text{(distance measurement)} \] 4. Specify the conditions for achieving the states "up" and    │
           │ "down" for the Gate based on the level to which the gate is lowered or raised: [ (gate\_state = down) \Rightarrow \text{(conditions for gate lowering)} \] [       │
           │ (gate\_state = up) \Rightarrow \text{(conditions for gate raising)} \] 5. Handshaking mechanism between the Train, Gate, and Controller components for signal      │
           │ exchange: - Train signals approaching: \( train\_state = near \) - Train signals crossing: \( train\_state = in \) - Gate lowers in response to approaching        │
           │ signal: \( gate\_state = down \) - Gate raises in response to crossing signal: \( gate\_state = up \) 6. Fail-safe mechanism for signal failure or                 │
           │ miscommunication: - Define behavior in case of signal failure for Train, Gate, and Controller components. 7. Include timings or thresholds for gate lowering and   │
           │ raising: - Specify time taken for gate to lower once signal is received. - Specify safe duration after train passes for gate to be raised. These invariants        │
           │ capture the essential requirements and constraints of the system, ensuring safe and efficient operation of the railroad crossing control system.                   │

           │ opens these gates after the train has sent a signal indicating that it crossed the road. The requirement that should be met by the control system is that the      │
           │ gates are always closed when the train is crossing the road. The complete system consists of the three components Train, Gate, and Controller: Train || Gate ||    │
           │ Controller.  Model this system. You can assume that all trains pass the relevant track section in the same direction.  Detail: The states of the transition system │
           │ for the Train have the following intuitive meaning: in state "far" the train is not close to the crossing, in state "near" it is approaching the crossing and has  │
           │ just sent a signal to notify this, and in state "in" it is at the crossing. The states "up" and "down" of the Gate have the obvious interpretation. The controller │
           │ has 4 states 0, 1, 2 and 3. It changes from state 0 to 1 when the train is approaching, from state 1 to 2 when the gate is lowering, from state 2 to 3 when the    │
           │ train is exiting the crossing, and from state 3 to 0 when the gate is raising. The state changes of the Controller stand for handshaking with the trains (via the  │
           │ actions approach and exit) and the Gate (via the actions lower and raise via which the Controller causes the gate to close or to open, respectively). Use the      │
           │ variable names train_state, controller_state and gate_state. [Hint 1] Clarify the appropriate states associated with each component in the task description to     │
           │ avoid confusion. Specify that the GateState should only include "up" and "down" states, which accurately represent the physical states of the gate (either open or │
           │ closed). The task description currently incorrectly assigns states such as "far", "in", and "near" to GateState, which are actually applicable to the TrainState.  │
           │ [Hint 2] Correct the state transition description for the Controller. The transitions should more clearly dictate the conditions under which the controller        │
           │ changes states to ensure the gate operates correctly. For instance, specify the condition that the controller should transition from state 0 to 1 only when the    │
           │ train state changes to "near" and that from state 1 to state 2 should only occur once the gate is fully down. [Hint 3] Ensure that the state "in" for the Train is │
           │ accurately described as the train being physically present at the crossing. This point should be emphasized to distinguish it clearly from the "near" state, in    │
           │ which the train is only approaching but not yet at the crossing. [Hint 4] Explicitly mention that the gate must be in the "down" state before the Controller can   │
           │ transition from state 1 to 2. This addition ensures that the gate's actual position is considered in the controller's logic, thereby preventing any unsafe         │
           │ scenarios where the gate might not be fully closed as the train passes. [Hint 5] Add a detailed description of conditions needed to transition the Controller from │
           │ state 2 to 3 and from state 3 to 0. Define clearly that the transition to state 3 can only happen after the train changes state from "in" to "far", and state 3 to │

           │ changes in train location and gate position to prevent any contradictions or unsafe behavior in transitioning logic. Make sure that you satisfy the following      │
           │ specifications: Based on the provided description and hints, here are the invariants for the system: 1. Gate is always closed (gate_state = "down") when the train │
